    \makeatletter
    \newsavebox\pandoc@box
    \newcommand*\pandocbounded[1]{%
      \sbox\pandoc@box{#1}%
      % scaling factors for width and height
      \Gscale@div\@tempa\textheight{\dimexpr\ht\pandoc@box+\dp\pandoc@box\relax}%
      \Gscale@div\@tempb\linewidth{\wd\pandoc@box}%
      % select the smaller of both
      \ifdim\@tempb\p@<\@tempa\p@
        \let\@tempa\@tempb
      \fi
      % scaling accordingly (\@tempa < 1)
      \ifdim\@tempa\p@<\p@
        \scalebox{\@tempa}{\usebox\pandoc@box}%
      % scaling not needed, use as it is
      \else
        \usebox{\pandoc@box}%
      \fi
    }
    \makeatother

\def\PY@reset{\let\PY@it=\relax \let\PY@bf=\relax%
    \let\PY@ul=\relax \let\PY@tc=\relax%
    \let\PY@bc=\relax \let\PY@ff=\relax}
\def\PY@tok#1{\csname PY@tok@#1\endcsname}
\def\PY@toks#1+{\ifx\relax#1\empty\else%
    \PY@tok{#1}\expandafter\PY@toks\fi}
\def\PY@do#1{\PY@bc{\PY@tc{\PY@ul{%
    \PY@it{\PY@bf{\PY@ff{#1}}}}}}}
\def\PY#1#2{\PY@reset\PY@toks#1+\relax+\PY@do{#2}}

    % For linebreaks inside Verbatim environment from package fancyvrb.
    \makeatletter
        \newbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox
        \newbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox
        \newcommand*\Wrappedvisiblespace {\textcolor{red}{\textvisiblespace}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol {\textcolor{red}{\llap{\tiny$\m@th\hookrightarrow$}}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationindent {3ex }
        \newcommand*\Wrappedafterbreak {\kern\Wrappedcontinuationindent\copy\Wrappedcontinuationbox}
        % Take advantage of the already applied Pygments mark-up to insert
        % potential linebreaks for TeX processing.
        %        {, <, #, %, $, ' and ": go to next line.
        %        _, }, ^, &, >, - and ~: stay at end of broken line.
        % Use of \textquotesingle for straight quote.
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatspecials {%
            \def\PYGZus{\discretionary{\char`\_}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\_}}%
            \def\PYGZob{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\{}{\char`\{}}%
            \def\PYGZcb{\discretionary{\char`\}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\}}}%
            \def\PYGZca{\discretionary{\char`\^}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\^}}%
            \def\PYGZam{\discretionary{\char`\&}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\&}}%
            \def\PYGZlt{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\<}{\char`\<}}%
            \def\PYGZgt{\discretionary{\char`\>}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\>}}%
            \def\PYGZsh{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\#}{\char`\#}}%
            \def\PYGZpc{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\%}{\char`\%}}%
            \def\PYGZdl{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\$}{\char`\$}}%
            \def\PYGZhy{\discretionary{\char`\-}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\-}}%
            \def\PYGZsq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\textquotesingle}{\textquotesingle}}%
            \def\PYGZdq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\"}{\char`\"}}%
            \def\PYGZti{\discretionary{\char`\~}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\~}}%
        }
        % Some characters . , ; ? ! / are not pygmentized.
        % This macro makes them "active" and they will insert potential linebreaks
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatpunct {%
            \lccode`\~`\.\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\.}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\.}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\,\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\,}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\,}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\;\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\;}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\;}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\:\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\:}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\:}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\?\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\?}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\?}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\!\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\!}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\!}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\/\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\/}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\/}}}%
            \catcode`\.\active
            \catcode`\,\active
            \catcode`\;\active
            \catcode`\:\active
            \catcode`\?\active
            \catcode`\!\active
            \catcode`\/\active
            \lccode`\~`\~
        }
    \makeatother

    \let\OriginalVerbatim=\Verbatim
    \makeatletter
    \renewcommand{\Verbatim}[1][1]{%
        %\parskip\z@skip
        \sbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox {\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol}%
        \sbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox {\FV@SetupFont\Wrappedvisiblespace}%
        \def\FancyVerbFormatLine ##1{\hsize\linewidth
            \vtop{\raggedright\hyphenpenalty\z@\exhyphenpenalty\z@
                \doublehyphendemerits\z@\finalhyphendemerits\z@
                \strut ##1\strut}%
        }%
        % If the linebreak is at a space, the latter will be displayed as visible
        % space at end of first line, and a continuation symbol starts next line.
        % Stretch/shrink are however usually zero for typewriter font.
        \def\FV@Space {%
            \nobreak\hskip\z@ plus\fontdimen3\font minus\fontdimen4\font
            \discretionary{\copy\Wrappedvisiblespacebox}{\Wrappedafterbreak}
            {\kern\fontdimen2\font}%
        }%

        % Allow breaks at special characters using \PYG... macros.
        \Wrappedbreaksatspecials
        % Breaks at punctuation characters . , ; ? ! and / need catcode=\active
        \OriginalVerbatim[#1,codes*=\Wrappedbreaksatpunct]%
    }
    \makeatother

\begin{document}
    
    \maketitle
    
    

    
    \section{End-to-End Exploratory Data
Analysis}\label{end-to-end-exploratory-data-analysis}

\subsection{Online Retail Dataset}\label{online-retail-dataset}

This project presents a complete and reproducible Exploratory Data
Analysis of the UCI Online Retail dataset.

\subsubsection{Project Objectives}\label{project-objectives}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Understand the dataset and its business context.
\item
  Assess data quality and investigate unusual observations.
\item
  Explore numerical, categorical, and temporal variables.
\item
  Investigate distributions and relationships.
\item
  Identify meaningful business patterns.
\item
  Communicate findings, limitations, and uncertainty.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Observational Unit}\label{observational-unit}

Each row represents an invoice line item, not necessarily an entire
invoice or a unique customer.

\subsubsection{Data Source}\label{data-source}

UCI Machine Learning Repository --- Online Retail

https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/352/online+retail

project paths, and prepare the output directories.

    \subsection{2. Visualization Convention}\label{visualization-convention}

This project uses Matplotlib's object-oriented API exclusively.

Figures are created using matplotlib.figure.Figure, and Axes are
obtained through the Figure's subplots() method.

IPython's display() function is used to publish figures immediately at
the point of execution.

The pyplot API is not used anywhere in this project.

    \subsection{3. Data Acquisition and Initial
Inspection}\label{data-acquisition-and-initial-inspection}

Before conducting any analysis, we must establish that the correct
dataset has been acquired and loaded successfully.

This section covers:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Verifying the integrity of the original dataset.
\item
  Loading the Excel workbook into Pandas.
\item
  Inspecting its structure and dimensions.
\item
  Examining variable names and data types.
\item
  Reviewing initial observations.
\end{enumerate}

The original dataset will remain unchanged. Any cleaning,
transformation, or derived data will be handled separately.

    \subsubsection{3.1 Data Integrity
Verification}\label{data-integrity-verification}

We use a SHA-256 checksum to verify that the local dataset matches the
file fingerprint recorded during acquisition.

This helps detect accidental modifications to the original workbook.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{3}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{k+kn}{import}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{hashlib}


\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{calculate\PYZus{}sha256}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{file\PYZus{}path}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{Path}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{o}{\PYZgt{}} \PY{n+nb}{str}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}Calculate the SHA\PYZhy{}256 checksum of a file.\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}

    \PY{n}{sha256} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{hashlib}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sha256}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{k}{with} \PY{n}{file\PYZus{}path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{open}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{rb}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{as} \PY{n}{file}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{chunk} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{iter}\PY{p}{(}\PY{k}{lambda}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{file}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{read}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{1024} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{1024}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+sa}{b}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{n}{sha256}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{update}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{chunk}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{sha256}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{hexdigest}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}


\PY{n}{actual\PYZus{}sha256} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculate\PYZus{}sha256}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{RAW\PYZus{}DATA\PYZus{}PATH}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Expected SHA\PYZhy{}256:}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{DATASET\PYZus{}SHA256}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Actual SHA\PYZhy{}256:  }\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{actual\PYZus{}sha256}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{actual\PYZus{}sha256} \PY{o}{!=} \PY{n}{DATASET\PYZus{}SHA256}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{k}{raise} \PY{n+ne}{ValueError}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Dataset integrity verification failed. }\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
        \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{The original dataset may have been modified.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s2}{Dataset integrity verified successfully.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
Expected SHA-256:
43465a06f2ccf7c8b5bd2892bc7defb52f97487934fe93b16ae4c3936424676d
Actual SHA-256:
43465a06f2ccf7c8b5bd2892bc7defb52f97487934fe93b16ae4c3936424676d

Dataset integrity verified successfully.
    \end{Verbatim}

    \subsubsection{3.2 Loading the Dataset}\label{loading-the-dataset}

We will load the original Excel workbook using Pandas.

Identifier columns will initially be represented as strings. Identifiers
are labels, not numerical measurements, even when their values contain
digits.

We will not perform any cleaning at this stage.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]

    \PY{n}{sheet\PYZus{}name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Online Retail}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{engine}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{openpyxl}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{\PYZob{}}
        \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{string}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{StockCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{string}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{CustomerID}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{string}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Dataset loaded successfully.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Shape: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ rows × }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ columns}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

The inspection includes:

\begin{itemize}

  Dataset dimensions.
\item
  Column names.
\item
  Data types.
\item
  Non-null counts.
\item
  Memory usage.
\end{itemize}

    \subsection{4. Data Quality Assessment}\label{data-quality-assessment}

Before conducting detailed exploratory analysis, we need to evaluate the
reliability and consistency of the dataset.

Data quality issues can distort statistical summaries, visualizations,
and business interpretations. Therefore, this section investigates
duplicate records, missing values, unusual numerical values, transaction
anomalies, temporal validity, and categorical inconsistencies.

The original dataset will remain unchanged throughout this
investigation. All identified issues will be documented before deciding
how they should be handled in subsequent analyses.

    \subsubsection{4.1 Duplicate Records}\label{duplicate-records}

Duplicate records may inflate transaction counts, quantities, and
transaction values.

We will investigate both:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Exact duplicate rows:} Records where every column has an
  identical value.
\item
  \textbf{Potential transaction-line duplicates:} Records sharing the
  same invoice number, stock code, description, quantity, unit price,
  customer, and invoice date.
\end{itemize}

An exact duplicate is not automatically proof of an erroneous record. We
must consider the dataset's observational unit and transaction structure
before deciding how to handle duplicates.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{9}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n}{exact\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}mask} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{duplicated}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{keep}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{exact\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}count} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{duplicated}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Total records: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Exact duplicate rows beyond first occurrences: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{exact\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Records involved in duplicate groups: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{exact\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}mask}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{exact\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}count} \PY{o}{\PYZgt{}} \PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s2}{Sample duplicate records:}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{display}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{loc}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{exact\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sort\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{by}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{columns}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{tolist}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{head}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{10}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsubsection{4.2 Missing Values}\label{missing-values}

Missing values can reduce the usable sample size and introduce bias,
particularly when their occurrence is not random.

We will examine:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Missing-value counts and percentages.
\item
  Missingness patterns across variables.
\item
  Whether records with missing customer identifiers still contain useful
  transaction information.
\end{itemize}

Missing values will not be automatically removed. Their treatment
depends on the analytical question and the role of each variable.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{10}{\boxspacing}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{missing\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{isna}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{missing\PYZus{}percentage}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{isna}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{mean}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{mul}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{100}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sort\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{missing\PYZus{}percentage}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ascending}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{4.3 Distribution of Duplicate
Records}\label{distribution-of-duplicate-records}

We have identified exact duplicate records, but we do not yet know
whether they are distributed uniformly or concentrated in particular
invoices.

We will examine the invoices containing the largest numbers of records
involved in exact-duplicate groups.

This helps us understand the scale and structure of the duplication
without prematurely deleting records.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{12}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n}{duplicate\PYZus{}invoice\PYZus{}summary} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{loc}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{exact\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{groupby}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{agg}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{n}{records\PYZus{}in\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}groups}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{size}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{distinct\PYZus{}stock\PYZus{}codes}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{StockCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{nunique}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{distinct\PYZus{}customers}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{CustomerID}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{nunique}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sort\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{records\PYZus{}in\PYZus{}duplicate\PYZus{}groups}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ascending}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{4.4 Numerical Validity and Unusual
Values}\label{numerical-validity-and-unusual-values}

The dataset contains two numerical transaction variables:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{Quantity}: Number of units recorded on an invoice line.
\item
  \texttt{UnitPrice}: Recorded price per unit.
\end{itemize}

We need to investigate:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Zero and negative quantities.
\item
  Zero and negative unit prices.
\item
  Extremely large positive or negative quantities.
\item
  Extremely high unit prices.
\end{itemize}

Negative quantities may represent returns or credit adjustments.
Zero-price records may represent samples, adjustments, or other special
transaction types.

These values are not automatically errors. Their business meaning must
be investigated before any exclusion or transformation.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{14}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n}{numerical\PYZus{}summary} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{UnitPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{describe}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{percentiles}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mf}{0.01}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.05}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.25}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.50}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.75}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.95}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.99}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{T}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{display}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{numerical\PYZus{}summary}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

        \PY{n}{df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

    \subsubsection{4.5 Invoice Types and Cancellation
Patterns}\label{invoice-types-and-cancellation-patterns}

Invoice numbers may contain prefixes that distinguish ordinary
transactions from cancellations or other special records.

We will investigate invoice-number patterns and compare them with
quantity signs.

In particular, we will examine whether invoices beginning with ``C'' are
associated with negative quantities.

This is an exploratory classification based on the recorded invoice
identifiers, not an assumption that every negative quantity must belong
to a cancellation.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{17}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]

    \PY{n}{df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{str}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{extract}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{r}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZca{}([A\PYZhy{}Za\PYZhy{}z]+)}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{expand}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{fillna}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Numeric}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{str}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{upper}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{invoice\PYZus{}prefix\PYZus{}summary} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{groupby}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{agg}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{n}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{size}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{unique\PYZus{}invoices}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{nunique}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{negative\PYZus{}quantity\PYZus{}records}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{k}{lambda} \PY{n}{s}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{lt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{positive\PYZus{}quantity\PYZus{}records}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{k}{lambda} \PY{n}{s}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{zero\PYZus{}quantity\PYZus{}records}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{k}{lambda} \PY{n}{s}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sort\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ascending}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{reset\PYZus{}index}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{invoice\PYZus{}prefix\PYZus{}summary}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}percentage}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}

    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{agg}\PY{p}{(}

    \section{5. Data Preparation and Analytical Dataset
Design}\label{data-preparation-and-analytical-dataset-design}

In this project, the Online Retail dataset contains several types of
records:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Ordinary merchandise transactions.
\item
  Cancellation and credit-note candidates.
\item
  Transactions with negative quantities.
\item
  Zero-price and negative-price records.
\item
  Financial and operational adjustments.
\item
  Records with incomplete customer or product information.
\item
  Exact duplicate records.
\end{itemize}

These observations cannot all be treated identically. Some represent
potentially legitimate business activity, while others require special
consideration depending on the analytical question.

\subsubsection{Objectives}\label{objectives}

This section will:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Preserve the original dataset and establish a separate analytical
  dataset.
\item
  Create useful analytical variables.
\item
  Calculate invoice-line values.
\item
  Classify transactions using observable characteristics.
\item
  Examine the resulting classifications.
\item
  Establish defensible analytical populations for subsequent analysis.
\item
  Validate and export the prepared analytical dataset.
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Data Preparation
Principles}\label{data-preparation-principles}

We will follow these principles throughout the project:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Preservation:} Never overwrite or silently modify the original
  source data.
\item
  \textbf{Transparency:} Document every transformation and analytical
  assumption.
\item
  \textbf{Contextual treatment:} Do not automatically classify unusual
  values as errors.
\item
  \textbf{Reproducibility:} Ensure that transformations can be repeated
  consistently.
\item
  \textbf{Analytical separation:} Different business questions may
  require different subsets of the same dataset.
\end{itemize}

The objective is not to create one universally cleaned dataset. It is to
create a reliable analytical foundation from which multiple
appropriately defined datasets can be derived.

    \subsection{5.1 Establishing the Analytical
Dataset}\label{establishing-the-analytical-dataset}

\subsubsection{Original Dataset vs.~Analytical
Dataset}\label{original-dataset-vs.-analytical-dataset}

The original dataset represents the information supplied by the data
provider. It should remain unchanged throughout the project.

The analytical dataset is a separate working copy that may contain
additional variables, classifications, and transformations.

This separation provides two important benefits:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  We can always return to the original observations and verify how a
  derived value was produced.
\item
  Different analytical transformations can be performed without
  contaminating the original data.
\end{enumerate}

We will use the following naming convention:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
DataFrame & Purpose \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{df\_raw} & Preserved copy of the original dataset \\
\texttt{df\_analysis} & Working analytical dataset \\
\end{longtable}
}

The analytical dataset will initially contain all 541,909 observations
and all eight original variables.

No records will be removed at this stage.

    \begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
Original dataset:
Shape: (541909, 8)

Analytical dataset:
Shape: (541909, 8)

Original columns:
    \end{Verbatim}

    
    \begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
        column           dtype
0    InvoiceNo          string
1    StockCode          string
2  Description          object
3     Quantity           int64
4  InvoiceDate  datetime64[us]
5    UnitPrice         float64
6   CustomerID          string
7      Country             str
    \end{Verbatim}

    
    \subsubsection{Interpretation}\label{interpretation}

The two DataFrames should have identical dimensions and original
columns.

At this stage:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  All 541,909 records are retained.
\item
  All eight original variables are preserved.
\item
  No missing values have been imputed.
\item
  No duplicate records have been removed.
\item
  No transaction has been excluded.
\end{itemize}

The analytical dataset is now ready for the creation of derived
variables.

    \subsection{5.2 Feature Engineering for Exploratory
Analysis}\label{feature-engineering-for-exploratory-analysis}

For this dataset, we will derive several variables.

\subsubsection{1. Invoice Prefix}\label{invoice-prefix}

Invoice numbers may contain alphabetic prefixes that distinguish
different transaction types.

We previously observed three categories:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Numeric invoice numbers.
\item
  C-prefixed invoice numbers.
\item
  A-prefixed invoice numbers.
\end{itemize}

We will extract the alphabetic prefix and use \texttt{NUMERIC} for
invoice numbers without one.

\subsubsection{2. Line Value}\label{line-value}

An invoice line's calculated value is:

{[} \text{LineValue}\_i = \text{Quantity}\_i \times \text{UnitPrice}\_i
{]}

This represents the extended value recorded for an individual invoice
line.

It is important to distinguish this measure from recognized accounting
revenue. The dataset does not provide a complete accounting definition
or all necessary financial adjustments.

\subsubsection{3. Date and Time
Components}\label{date-and-time-components}

The invoice timestamp can be decomposed into useful temporal variables:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Year
\item
  Month
\item
  Day
\item
  Day of week
\item
  Hour
\item
  Weekend indicator
\end{itemize}

These variables will support subsequent temporal analysis.

\subsubsection{4. Analytical Flags}\label{analytical-flags}

We will create Boolean variables identifying:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Positive, negative, and zero quantities.
\item
  Positive, negative, and zero unit prices.
\item
  Missing customer identifiers.
\item
  Missing product descriptions.
\item
  Known operational stock codes.
\end{itemize}

These flags will allow us to investigate overlapping characteristics
without forcing every record into a single category.

    \subsubsection{5.2.1 Extracting Invoice
Prefixes}\label{extracting-invoice-prefixes}

We will extract alphabetic characters appearing at the beginning of each
invoice number.

If no alphabetic prefix exists, the value will be \texttt{NUMERIC}.

All extracted prefixes will be converted to uppercase for consistency.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{32}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{str}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{extract}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{r}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZca{}([A\PYZhy{}Za\PYZhy{}z]+)}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{expand}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{fillna}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{NUMERIC}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{str}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{upper}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Invoice prefixes:}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{display}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{value\PYZus{}counts}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{rename\PYZus{}axis}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{reset\PYZus{}index}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \end{Verbatim}

    \end{Verbatim}

    
    \subsubsection{5.2.2 Calculating Invoice-Line
Values}\label{calculating-invoice-line-values}

For every invoice line, we calculate the product of its quantity and
unit price.

The resulting variable is called \texttt{LineValue}.

Its interpretation depends on the signs of the underlying variables:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2667}}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2667}}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2667}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Quantity
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Unit Price
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Line Value
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Basic interpretation
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Positive & Positive & Positive & Positive-valued transaction line \\
Negative & Positive & Negative & Negative quantity adjustment \\
Positive & Negative & Negative & Negative price adjustment \\
Negative & Negative & Positive & Both quantity and price are negative \\
Any & Zero & Zero & Zero-valued transaction line \\
\end{longtable}
}

A negative line value is not automatically an error. It may represent a
cancellation, return, or financial adjustment.

We will preserve the calculated values without modifying their signs.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]

    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{UnitPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{LineValue calculated successfully.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{display}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}
        \PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{UnitPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{LineValue}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{head}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{10}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s2}{Line\PYZhy{}value summary:}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{5.2.3 Extracting Temporal
Components}\label{extracting-temporal-components}

The original \texttt{InvoiceDate} variable contains both date and time
information.

We will derive separate components to make temporal grouping and
comparison easier.

The \texttt{DayOfWeek} variable will use the full weekday name, while
\texttt{DayOfWeekNumber} will use Monday = 0 through Sunday = 6.

The weekend indicator will identify Saturday and Sunday.

These variables are analytical conveniences. They do not imply that the
retailer operated every day or that every recorded transaction
represents normal trading activity.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]

\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceYear}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{invoice\PYZus{}dates}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{year}
\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceMonth}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{invoice\PYZus{}dates}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{month}
\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceDay}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{invoice\PYZus{}dates}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{day}

\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{DayOfWeekNumber}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{invoice\PYZus{}dates}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{day\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}week}
\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{DayOfWeek}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{invoice\PYZus{}dates}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{day\PYZus{}name}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceHour}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{invoice\PYZus{}dates}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{hour}

\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{temporal\PYZus{}columns} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceDate}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}

    \subsubsection{5.2.4 Defining Operational Stock
Codes}\label{defining-operational-stock-codes}

Our data-quality investigation identified five stock codes that
represent operational or non-standard merchandise entries:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Stock code & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{B} & Adjust bad debt \\
\texttt{M} & Manual \\
\texttt{AMAZONFEE} & Amazon fee \\
\texttt{POST} & Postage \\
\texttt{DOT} & Dotcom postage \\
\end{longtable}
}

These codes should not automatically be interpreted as ordinary physical
products.

We will create a Boolean variable named \texttt{IsOperationalCode} to
identify them.

This is a deliberately narrow classification based on the five codes
observed during our investigation. It does not imply that every other
stock code necessarily represents a conventional merchandise product.

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{POST}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{DOT}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}

\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{StockCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{isin}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{OPERATIONAL\PYZus{}STOCK\PYZus{}CODES}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Operational\PYZhy{}code records:}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{5.2.5 Creating Diagnostic
Flags}\label{creating-diagnostic-flags}

We will now create independent Boolean flags for important data
characteristics.

A record can satisfy several conditions simultaneously. For example, a
C-prefixed invoice may also have a negative quantity and a positive unit
price.

These flags are therefore not mutually exclusive.

This distinction matters because a single categorical variable cannot
adequately represent every characteristic of a transaction.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{36}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Quantity characteristics}
\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsNegativeQuantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{lt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsZeroQuantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsection{5.3 Transaction
Classification}\label{transaction-classification}

The dataset contains different types of transaction records. Treating
every observation as an ordinary merchandise sale would produce
misleading results.

For example:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  A C-prefixed invoice has a negative quantity in every observed record.
\item
  An A-prefixed invoice represents a bad-debt adjustment in the three
  observed records.
\item
  Some stock codes represent fees, postage, or manual entries.
\item
  Negative quantities also occur on numeric invoices.
\end{itemize}

We therefore need a systematic way to distinguish these records.

\subsubsection{Primary Transaction
Categories}\label{primary-transaction-categories}

We will assign each record one primary category using the following
classification hierarchy:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Priority
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Condition
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Primary category
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
1 & Invoice prefix is A & Bad-debt adjustment \\
2 & Invoice prefix is C & Cancellation / credit-note candidate \\
3 & Stock code is a known operational code & Operational /
non-merchandise \\
4 & Quantity is negative & Negative-quantity transaction \\
5 & Unit price is negative & Negative-price adjustment \\
6 & Unit price is zero & Zero-price transaction \\
7 & Quantity and unit price are positive & Positive-priced
transaction \\
8 & None of the above & Other / review required \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsubsection{Why Classification Order
Matters}\label{why-classification-order-matters}

The conditions are evaluated in priority order. The first matching
condition determines the primary category.

For example, a negative-quantity record with a C-prefixed invoice will
be classified as a cancellation candidate rather than a generic
negative-quantity transaction.

This does not discard its negative-quantity characteristic. That
characteristic remains available through the independent Boolean flags
created earlier.

The hierarchy is an analytical convention, not a definitive accounting
classification.

    \subsubsection{5.3.1 Assigning Primary Transaction
Categories}\label{assigning-primary-transaction-categories}

We will use NumPy's \texttt{select()} function to implement the
classification hierarchy.

The function evaluates a sequence of Boolean conditions and assigns the
corresponding category for the first condition that evaluates to
\texttt{True}.

Records that do not match any specified condition will receive
\texttt{Other\ /\ review\ required}.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{38}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n}{conditions} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{A}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{C}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsNegativeQuantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsNegativePrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsZeroPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{p}{(}
        \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsPositiveQuantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsPositivePrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{categories} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Bad\PYZhy{}debt adjustment}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Cancellation / credit\PYZhy{}note candidate}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Operational / non\PYZhy{}merchandise}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Negative\PYZhy{}quantity transaction}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Negative\PYZhy{}price adjustment}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Zero\PYZhy{}price transaction}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Positive\PYZhy{}priced transaction}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{TransactionCategory}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{select}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{conditions}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{categories}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{default}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Other / review required}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Transaction classification completed.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s2}{Assigned categories:}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{display}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{TransactionCategory}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{value\PYZus{}counts}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{rename\PYZus{}axis}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{TransactionCategory}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{reset\PYZus{}index}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsubsection{5.3.2 Summarizing Transaction
Categories}\label{summarizing-transaction-categories}

A category count alone does not tell us everything about a transaction
group.

We will summarize each category using:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Number of records.
\item
  Number of unique invoices.
\item
  Number of unique stock codes.
\item
  Total recorded quantity.
\item
  Total calculated line value.
\item
  Number of records with an identified customer.
\item
  Percentage of the full dataset.
\end{itemize}

This provides an initial quantitative comparison of the transaction
categories.

\textbf{Important:} These totals are descriptive summaries, not final
sales or revenue measures. Their interpretation depends on the
transaction types included in each category.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{39}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]

    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{groupby}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{TransactionCategory}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{agg}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{n}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{size}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{unique\PYZus{}invoices}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoiceNo}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{nunique}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{unique\PYZus{}stock\PYZus{}codes}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{StockCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{nunique}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{total\PYZus{}quantity}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{sum}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{total\PYZus{}line\PYZus{}value}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{LineValue}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{sum}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{records\PYZus{}with\PYZus{}customer\PYZus{}id}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{HasCustomerID}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{sum}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sort\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ascending}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{reset\PYZus{}index}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{transaction\PYZus{}category\PYZus{}summary}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}percentage}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{transaction\PYZus{}category\PYZus{}summary}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{div}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{mul}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{100}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{5.3.3 Cross-Validating Transaction
Classifications}\label{cross-validating-transaction-classifications}

A classification hierarchy should be examined rather than accepted
blindly.

We will compare invoice prefixes against the assigned primary
categories.

This cross-tabulation helps verify that:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  A-prefixed invoices are classified as bad-debt adjustments.
\item
  C-prefixed invoices are classified as cancellation candidates.
\item
  Numeric invoices with negative quantities remain distinguishable.
\item
  Operational stock codes are not incorrectly treated as ordinary
  merchandise.
\end{itemize}

The table also helps identify unexpected combinations that may require
further investigation.

\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{5.3.4 Reviewing Independent Diagnostic
Flags}\label{reviewing-independent-diagnostic-flags}

The primary transaction category provides a convenient summary, but it
cannot describe every characteristic of a record.

We will therefore summarize the independent diagnostic flags separately.

This is particularly important for identifying overlapping conditions,
such as operational records with negative quantities or zero prices.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{41}{\boxspacing}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsNegativeQuantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsPositiveQuantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsNegativePrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsZeroPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsPositivePrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{HasCustomerID}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{HasDescription}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{diagnostic\PYZus{}flag\PYZus{}summary} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{DataFrame}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{\PYZob{}}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{flag}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{diagnostic\PYZus{}flag\PYZus{}columns}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{p}{[}
        \PY{n+nb}{int}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{column}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{column} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{diagnostic\PYZus{}flag\PYZus{}columns}
    \PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{diagnostic\PYZus{}flag\PYZus{}summary}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{percentage}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{diagnostic\PYZus{}flag\PYZus{}summary}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{record\PYZus{}count}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{div}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{Interpretation of Transaction
Classification}\label{interpretation-of-transaction-classification}

The preceding tables provide the evidence needed to assess the
classification.

In particular, we should examine:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Whether the category counts account for all records.
\item
  Whether the prefix-category relationships match our observed invoice
  patterns.
\item
  How many numeric invoices contain negative quantities.
\item
  How operational records overlap with unusual quantity and price
  values.
\item
  Whether any records remain in the review-required category.
\end{enumerate}

We will document the actual findings after inspecting the outputs.

No transaction category will be treated as a definitive measure of
recognized sales or revenue merely because of its label.

    \subsection{5.4 Data Integrity and Preservation
Validation}\label{data-integrity-and-preservation-validation}

After creating derived variables and transaction classifications, we
must verify that the preparation process has not unintentionally
modified the original observations.

A reliable analytical workflow should be able to demonstrate that:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The original record count is unchanged.
\item
  Every original variable is preserved.
\item
  Original values are identical to those in the preserved dataset.
\item
  Derived calculations follow their documented definitions.
\item
  The primary transaction categories cover every record exactly once.
\end{itemize}

These checks are important because a dataset can appear analytically
convenient while silently losing or altering information.

    \subsubsection{5.4.1 Validating Original
Columns}\label{validating-original-columns}

We will compare the original columns in the analytical DataFrame with
the preserved reference DataFrame.

The comparison must be exact, including data types, index, missing
values, and values.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{42}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{==} \PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}raw}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{(}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Record count changed during preparation.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n+nb}{all}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{column} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{columns}
    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{column} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{RAW\PYZus{}COLUMNS}
\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{One or more original columns are missing.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}

\PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{testing}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{assert\PYZus{}frame\PYZus{}equal}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{RAW\PYZus{}COLUMNS}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}raw}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Original data preservation: PASSED}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Record count preservation: PASSED}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Original values and data types: PASSED}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsubsection{5.4.2 Validating Derived

Next, we will independently verify selected derived variables against
their definitions.

These assertions help detect accidental changes to the transformation
logic.

    \subsection{5.5 Exporting the Prepared Analytical
Dataset}\label{exporting-the-prepared-analytical-dataset}

The analytical dataset now contains the original observations together
with derived variables and transaction classifications.

We will save it separately from the original source workbook.

\subsubsection{File Format: Parquet}\label{file-format-parquet}

We will use the Parquet format for the prepared analytical dataset.

Parquet is a columnar storage format that offers several advantages:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Efficient compression and storage.
\item
  Preservation of data types.
\item
  Efficient reading of selected columns.
\item
  Compatibility with common Python data-analysis tools.
\end{itemize}

The original Excel workbook will remain unchanged.

We will also export the transaction-category summary as CSV because it
is convenient to inspect and share.

    \subsubsection{5.5.1 Checking Parquet
Support}\label{checking-parquet-support}

Pandas requires an additional Parquet engine, such as PyArrow, to write
Parquet files.

PyArrow is not currently listed among the project's dependencies. We
will add it to the project environment before exporting the analytical
dataset.

This ensures that the notebook's dependencies are explicitly documented
and reproducible.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{45}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{k+kn}{import}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{importlib}\PY{n+nn}{.}\PY{n+nn}{util}

\PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{importlib}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{util}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{find\PYZus{}spec}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{pyarrow}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o+ow}{is} \PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{k}{raise} \PY{n+ne}{ImportError}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{PyArrow is not installed. }\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
        \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Run `uv add pyarrow` in the project directory, }\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
        \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{then restart the notebook kernel.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Parquet engine available: PyArrow}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsubsection{5.5.3 Verifying the Exported
Dataset}\label{verifying-the-exported-dataset}

After exporting the analytical dataset to Parquet, we reload it and
verify that the exported data preserves the original analytical dataset.

The verification checks:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Dataset dimensions.
\item
  Column names and order.
\item
  Index.
\item
  Exact data values.
\end{itemize}

We do not require identical dtype metadata because Parquet serialization
may represent Pandas nullable-string metadata differently. The values
themselves must remain exactly equal.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{47}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Verify the exported dataset.}
\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}exported} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{read\PYZus{}parquet}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{ANALYTICAL\PYZus{}DATA\PYZus{}PATH}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Verify dimensions, column order, and index.}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}exported}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape} \PY{o}{==} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}exported}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{columns}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{equals}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{columns}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}exported}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{index}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{equals}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{index}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Normalize Description values for a fair comparison.}
\PY{n}{original} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Description}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{astype}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{string}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{exported} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}exported}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Description}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{astype}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{string}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{testing}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{assert\PYZus{}series\PYZus{}equal}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{original}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{exported}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{check\PYZus{}dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{check\PYZus{}exact}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Compare all remaining columns.}
\PY{n}{other\PYZus{}columns} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}
    \PY{n}{column} \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{column} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{columns}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{column} \PY{o}{!=} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Description}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{testing}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{assert\PYZus{}frame\PYZus{}equal}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{other\PYZus{}columns}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}exported}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{other\PYZus{}columns}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{check\PYZus{}dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{check\PYZus{}exact}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Parquet export verification: PASSED}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Exported shape: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}exported}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{File size: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{ANALYTICAL\PYZus{}DATA\PYZus{}PATH}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{stat}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{st\PYZus{}size}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ bytes}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{All values match after normalizing Description text.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsection{5.6 Section Summary}\label{section-summary}

In this section, we established a separate analytical dataset while
preserving the original observations.

\subsubsection{Work Completed}\label{work-completed}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Created \texttt{df\_raw} as a preserved reference to the original
  eight-column dataset.
\item
  Created \texttt{df\_analysis} as the working analytical dataset.
\item
  Extracted invoice prefixes.
\item
  Calculated invoice-line values.
\item
  Derived temporal variables from invoice timestamps.
\item
  Identified known operational stock codes.
\item
  Created independent diagnostic flags for quantities, prices, and
  missing values.
\item
  Assigned primary transaction categories using a documented
  classification hierarchy.
\item
  Summarized and cross-validated the resulting categories.
\item
  Verified the integrity of the original data and derived variables.
\item
  Exported the prepared analytical dataset and transaction-category
  summary.
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Important Analytical
Decisions}\label{important-analytical-decisions}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  No original records were deleted.
\item
  Missing customer identifiers and descriptions were retained.
\item
  Negative quantities were not automatically interpreted as customer
  returns.
\item
  C-prefixed invoices were classified as cancellation / credit-note
  candidates based on observed evidence.
\item
  Operational stock codes were distinguished from ordinary merchandise
  codes.
\item
  Calculated line values were not automatically treated as recognized
  accounting revenue.
\item
  The primary transaction classification was kept separate from
  independent diagnostic flags.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Analytical Limitations}\label{analytical-limitations}

The dataset does not provide a complete accounting definition of sales,
returns, discounts, or revenue recognition.

Consequently, subsequent analyses must define their populations and
metrics explicitly. Different business questions may require different
subsets of the analytical dataset.

The classifications created here are analytical tools, not definitive
statements about the retailer's accounting system.

    \subsubsection{5.7.1 Candidate Analytical Population
Definitions}\label{candidate-analytical-population-definitions}

We will evaluate six candidate populations.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Candidate
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Definition
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Main analytical focus
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
All transactions & All available records & Complete operational
overview \\
Merchandise transactions & Excludes records identified by known
operational stock codes & Broad merchandise activity \\
Positive-priced merchandise & Merchandise records with UnitPrice
\textgreater{} 0 & Merchandise activity, including cancellation-related
lines \\
Positive sales candidates & Merchandise records with positive Quantity
and UnitPrice, and numeric invoice numbers & Positive merchandise sales
candidates \\
Customer-identifiable merchandise & Merchandise records with an
available CustomerID & Customer-linked merchandise activity \\
Customer-identifiable positive sales candidates & Positive sales
candidates with an available CustomerID & Customer and sales analysis \\
\end{longtable}
}

These definitions are analytical conventions, not proof of completed
payment or fulfillment.

The original dataset will remain unchanged. Each candidate population
will be created using a Boolean filter on the prepared analytical
dataset.

    \subsubsection{5.7.2 Evaluation Criteria}\label{evaluation-criteria}

We will compare the six populations using the following dimensions:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Record coverage:} Number and percentage of records retained.
\item
  \textbf{Invoice coverage:} Number of distinct invoices represented.
\item
  \textbf{Product coverage:} Number of distinct stock codes represented.
\item
  \textbf{Customer coverage:} Number of identifiable customers
  represented.
\item
  \textbf{Temporal coverage:} Earliest and latest transaction dates.
\item
  \textbf{Geographic coverage:} Number of countries represented.
\item
  \textbf{Data completeness:} Availability of CustomerID and
  Description.
\item
  \textbf{Financial and quantity coverage:} Total quantity and total
  invoice-line value.
\end{enumerate}

No single metric determines suitability. A smaller population may be
more appropriate for a focused analysis, while a broader population may
preserve more useful information.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{48}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n}{candidate\PYZus{}populations} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{\PYZob{}}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{All transactions}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Series}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{index}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{index}
    \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Merchandise transactions}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{p}{(}
        \PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Positive\PYZhy{}priced merchandise}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{p}{(}
        \PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{UnitPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Positive sales candidates}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{p}{(}
        \PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{UnitPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{NUMERIC}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Customer\PYZhy{}identifiable merchandise}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{p}{(}
        \PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{HasCustomerID}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Customer\PYZhy{}identifiable positive sales candidates}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{p}{(}
        \PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{UnitPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{NUMERIC}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{o}{\PYZam{}} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{HasCustomerID}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}

\PY{n}{population\PYZus{}results} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{]}

\PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{population\PYZus{}name}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{mask} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{candidate\PYZus{}populations}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{items}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{subset} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{loc}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{mask}\PY{p}{]}

    \PY{n}{population\PYZus{}results}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{append}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{\PYZob{}}

    \subsubsection{5.7.3 Interpreting Candidate Population
Coverage}\label{interpreting-candidate-population-coverage}

The comparison table provides the quantitative foundation for selecting
our analytical population.

We should pay particular attention to:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  How much of the original dataset each candidate retains.
\item
  Whether the population preserves sufficient product, invoice,
  customer, temporal, and geographic diversity.
\item
  Whether missing CustomerID values materially restrict customer-level
  analysis.
\item
  Whether cancellation-related and operational records introduce
  complications for the intended analysis.
\item
  Whether a narrower population sacrifices useful information without
  providing a meaningful analytical advantage.
\end{itemize}

Importantly, total line value is a descriptive measure of the selected
records. It should not automatically be interpreted as recognized
revenue.

    \subsubsection{5.7.4 Selecting the Primary Analytical
Population}\label{selecting-the-primary-analytical-population}

After evaluating all six candidate populations, we select
\textbf{Positive Sales Candidates} as the primary analytical population
for this project.

The population consists of records satisfying all the following
conditions:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  The stock code is not identified as a known
  operational/non-merchandise code.
\item
  Quantity is strictly positive.
\item
  UnitPrice is strictly positive.
\item
  Invoice number has a numeric prefix.
\end{enumerate}

This selection retains 527,948 records, representing approximately
97.424\% of the original dataset.

The selected population provides a strong balance between analytical
clarity and data coverage. It preserves substantial product, invoice,
customer, temporal, and geographic diversity while excluding known
operational records and non-positive merchandise lines.

Missing CustomerID values will be retained. They do not prevent the
corresponding records from contributing to product, temporal,
geographic, and general transaction analysis. Customer-level analyses
will use only records with an identifiable CustomerID.

\textbf{Important limitation:} These records are positive sales
candidates, not independently verified completed or paid sales. The
dataset does not establish payment or fulfillment status.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{49}{\boxspacing}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Positive sales candidates}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{loc}\PY{p}{[}
    \PY{n}{PRIMARY\PYZus{}POPULATION\PYZus{}MASK}
\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{copy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Selected records: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Coverage: }\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{/}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}analysis}\PY{p}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{100}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.3f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZpc{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{==} \PY{l+m+mi}{527\PYZus{}948}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{all}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{UnitPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{all}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{p}{(}\PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{all}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{NUMERIC}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{all}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{5.7.6 Population Selection
Summary}\label{population-selection-summary}

The primary analytical DataFrame is now \texttt{df\_eda}.

It contains the selected positive sales candidates while preserving the
original prepared dataset in \texttt{df\_analysis}.

We will use \texttt{df\_eda} as the starting population for the
subsequent exploratory analysis.

When an analytical question requires customer identity, we will create a
customer-identifiable analytical subset from \texttt{df\_eda} rather
than changing the primary population.

This distinction allows us to maintain a consistent primary population
while applying appropriate eligibility criteria to individual analyses.

    \subsubsection{5.7.7 Validating the Selected Analytical
Population}\label{validating-the-selected-analytical-population}

Before proceeding to exploratory analysis, we will validate the selected
DataFrame.

The validation will confirm that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  The selected population contains the expected number of records.
\item
  Every record has a positive quantity and positive unit price.
\item
  No known operational stock codes are included.
\item
  Every invoice number has a numeric prefix.
\item
  The selected DataFrame matches the corresponding records in the
  prepared dataset.
\item
  Missing CustomerID values have been retained rather than inadvertently
  discarded.
\end{enumerate}

These checks ensure that our analytical population follows the
definition established in Section 5.7.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{50}{\boxspacing}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Validate population size.}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{==} \PY{l+m+mi}{527\PYZus{}948}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Validate selection criteria.}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Quantity}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{all}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{UnitPrice}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{gt}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{all}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{p}{(}\PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{IsOperationalCode}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{all}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{assert} \PY{n}{df\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{InvoicePrefix}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eq}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{NUMERIC}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{all}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Validate population consistency.}

\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{testing}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{assert\PYZus{}frame\PYZus{}equal}\PY{p}{(}

    \PY{n}{expected\PYZus{}eda}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{5.7.8 Profiling the Selected Analytical

We will generate a concise profile of the final analytical population.

This profile records its size, coverage, distinct entities, missing
values, and temporal boundaries. It will serve as a reference when
interpreting subsequent analytical results.

    \subsubsection{5.7.9 Exporting the Selected Analytical
Population}\label{exporting-the-selected-analytical-population}

We will save the selected population as a separate Parquet file.

The prepared analytical dataset and the selected EDA dataset serve
different purposes:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{online\_retail\_analytical.parquet}: The complete prepared
  dataset, including all transaction categories.
\item
  \texttt{online\_retail\_eda.parquet}: The selected primary analytical
  population.
\end{itemize}

Keeping both datasets allows us to reproduce the population selection
and revisit excluded records if necessary.

\PY{p}{)}
